I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory optuna__optuna__3.0. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
CmaEsSampler uses independent sampler even for non-dynamic search spaces
### Expected behavior

I am using a clearly non-dynamic search space with the `CmaEsSampler` but still see warnings that the independent sampler is triggered very frequently.

I investigated this with a debugger and found that `CmaEsSampler` sometimes (or in my case, frequently) samples values that are just a bit outside of the distribution bounds. `Trial._is_relative_param()` then checks whether the value sampled for the relative search space is within bounds and, if not, the independent sampler is triggered.

#1557 seems to be a related issue.

My `Trial.relative_search_space` looks as follows:

```python
{
    'initial_learning_rate': LogUniformDistribution(high=0.001, low=1e-05),
    'learning_rate_decay_factor': UniformDistribution(high=0.9999, low=0.8),
    'base_filter_size_ln2': IntUniformDistribution(high=6, low=3, step=1),
    'block_size': IntUniformDistribution(high=2, low=1, step=1),
    'bottleneck_size': IntUniformDistribution(high=150, low=10, step=10),
    'num_blocks': IntUniformDistribution(high=5, low=2, step=1),
    'num_downsampling_steps': IntUniformDistribution(high=7, low=3, step=1),
    'reg_scale_l1': LogUniformDistribution(high=0.1, low=0.0001),
    'number_of_epochs': IntUniformDistribution(high=1000, low=500, step=100),
    'weight_decay': LogUniformDistribution(high=0.001, low=1e-06)
}
```

And the trial I investigated with the debugger had the following `Trial.relative_params` sampled with CmaEsSampler:

```python
{
    'initial_learning_rate': 9.999999999999997e-06,
    'learning_rate_decay_factor': 0.9998999999999999,
    'base_filter_size_ln2': 7,
    'block_size': 1,
    'bottleneck_size': 150,
    'num_blocks': 2,
    'num_downsampling_steps': 3,
    'reg_scale_l1': 0.00010000000000000009,
    'number_of_epochs': 500,
    'weight_decay': 0.0009999999999999998
}
```

We can see that some sampled values are slightly out of bounds:

- `initial_learning_rate` is 9.999e-06 < 1e-05.
- `base_filter_size_ln2` is 7 > 6.

This happens on almost every trial for me. Especially the integer parameters are prone to this.
Is this a bug or do I need to make my search space less restricted?

### Environment

- Optuna version: 2.10.0
- Python version: 3.8.12
- OS: Linux-5.4.0-94-generic-x86_64-with-glibc2.10
- (Optional) Other libraries and their versions:
  - cmaes: 0.8.2

### Steps to reproduce

Admittedly, the following MWE is not optimal, but I could reproduce the behavior with it at least one out of 100 times:

```python
import optuna

class MWE:
    def __call__(self, trial):
        num = trial.suggest_int("num", 0, 15, step=1)
        denom = trial.suggest_int("denom", 1, 2, step=1)
        x = num / denom
        return 6 * (x**4) - 14 * (x**3) - 2 * (x**2) + 10 * x

if __name__ == "__main__":
    optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)
    objective = MWE()

    for seed in range(100):
        study = optuna.create_study(
            sampler=optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler(seed=seed)
        )
        study.optimize(objective, n_trials=500)
        print("Best value: {} (params: {})\n".format(study.best_value, study.best_params))
```

This yielded:

```

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read and understand the issue:

1. The problem is that CmaEsSampler frequently triggers the independent sampler even for non-dynamic search spaces
2. This happens because CmaEsSampler sometimes samples values that are slightly outside of distribution bounds
3. Trial._is_relative_param() checks if sampled values are within bounds, and if not, triggers the independent sampler
4. The issue shows examples where:
   - initial_learning_rate: 9.999e-06 < 1e-05 (slightly below lower bound)
   - base_filter_size_ln2: 7 > 6 (slightly above upper bound)
5. This affects both float and integer parameters, especially integer parameters
6. The warning messages indicate that parameters are being sampled independently by TPESampler or RandomSampler instead of CmaEsSampler

Key technical details:
- Class: CmaEsSampler
- Method involved: Trial._is_relative_param()
- Distributions affected: LogUniformDistribution, UniformDistribution, IntUniformDistribution
- Issue #1557 is related

The core issue seems to be numerical precision errors in the CMA-ES algorithm causing sampled values to slightly exceed distribution bounds, triggering fallback to independent sampling unnecessarily.

Steps to reproduce:
1. Create a study with CmaEsSampler
2. Define parameters with various distributions (especially integer ones)
3. Run optimization - observe frequent warnings about independent sampling

Best practices for fixing:
- Need to handle numerical precision issues gracefully
- Should clamp or round values to stay within bounds instead of falling back to independent sampling
- Minimal changes to preserve existing functionality
- Ensure mathematical correctness while improving robustness</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1 Problem Analysis

The issue describes that `CmaEsSampler` incorrectly triggers the independent sampler for non-dynamic search spaces due to numerical precision errors. The CMA-ES algorithm samples values that are slightly outside distribution bounds, causing `Trial._is_relative_param()` to reject them and fall back to independent sampling.

### 1.2 Technical Details Highlighted

- **Class**: `CmaEsSampler`
- **Method**: `Trial._is_relative_param()`
- **Error Messages**: Warnings about parameters being sampled independently
- **Distributions**: `LogUniformDistribution`, `UniformDistribution`, `IntUniformDistribution`
- **Related Issue**: #1557
- **Library**: cmaes version 0.8.2

### 1.3 Clear Problem Statement

The `CmaEsSampler` produces parameter values that slightly exceed distribution boundaries due to floating-point precision errors. These out-of-bounds values trigger the independent sampler fallback mechanism, degrading optimization performance even though the search space is static and supported.

### 1.4 Reproduction Steps

1. Create a study with `CmaEsSampler`
2. Define parameters with bounded distributions (especially integers)
3. Run optimization 
4. Observe frequent warnings about independent sampling

### 1.5 Best Practices for Fix

- Handle numerical precision gracefully
- Clamp or round values to stay within bounds
- Preserve mathematical correctness
- Make minimal changes to existing functionality
- Ensure backward compatibility

     3	# Optuna: A hyperparameter optimization framework
     4	
     5	[![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.6%20%7C%203.7%20%7C%203.8%20%7C%203.9-blue)](https://www.python.org)
     6	[![pypi](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/optuna.svg)](https://pypi.python.org/pypi/optuna)
     7	[![conda](https://img.shields.io/conda/vn/conda-forge/optuna.svg)](https://anaconda.org/conda-forge/optuna)
     8	[![GitHub license](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-blue.svg)](https://github.com/optuna/optuna)
     9	[![CircleCI](https://circleci.com/gh/optuna/optuna.svg?style=svg)](https://circleci.com/gh/optuna/optuna)
    10	[![Read the Docs](https://readthedocs.org/projects/optuna/badge/?version=stable)](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/)
    11	[![Codecov](https://codecov.io/gh/optuna/optuna/branch/master/graph/badge.svg)](https://codecov.io/gh/optuna/optuna/branch/master)
    12	[![Gitter chat](https://badges.gitter.im/optuna/gitter.svg)](https://gitter.im/optuna/optuna)
    13	
    14	[**Website**](https://optuna.org/)
    15	| [**Docs**](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/)
    16	| [**Install Guide**](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/installation.html)
    17	| [**Tutorial**](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/tutorial/index.html)
    18	
    19	*Optuna* is an automatic hyperparameter optimization software framework, particularly designed
    20	for machine learning. It features an imperative, *define-by-run* style user API. Thanks to our
    21	*define-by-run* API, the code written with Optuna enjoys high modularity, and the user of
    22	Optuna can dynamically construct the search spaces for the hyperparameters.
    23

    34	## Key Features
    35	
    36	Optuna has modern functionalities as follows:
    37	
    38	- [Lightweight, versatile, and platform agnostic architecture](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/tutorial/10_key_features/001_first.html)
    39	  - Handle a wide variety of tasks with a simple installation that has few requirements.
    40	- [Pythonic search spaces](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/tutorial/10_key_features/002_configurations.html)
    41	  - Define search spaces using familiar Python syntax including conditionals and loops.
    42	- [Efficient optimization algorithms](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/tutorial/10_key_features/003_efficient_optimization_algorithms.html)
    43	  - Adopt state-of-the-art algorithms for sampling hyperparameters and efficiently pruning unpromising trials.
    44	- [Easy parallelization](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/tutorial/10_key_features/004_distributed.html)
    45	  - Scale studies to tens or hundreds or workers with little or no changes to the code.
    46	- [Quick visualization](https://optuna.readthedocs.io/en/stable/tutorial/10_key_features/005_visualization.html)
    47	  - Inspect optimization histories from a variety of plotting functions.
    48	
    49	
    50	## Basic Concepts
    51	
    52	We use the terms *study* and *trial* as follows:
    53	
    54	- Study: optimization based on an objective function
    55	- Trial: a single execution of the objective function
    56	
    57	Please refer to sample code below. The goal of a *study* is to find out the optimal set of
    58	hyperparameter values (e.g., `classifier` and `svm_c`) through multiple *trials* (e.g.,
    59	`n_trials=100`). Optuna is a framework designed for the automation and the acceleration of the
    60	optimization *studies*.
    61	
    62	[![Open in Colab](https://colab.research.google.com/assets/colab-badge.svg)](http://colab.research.google.com/github/optuna/optuna-examples/blob/main/quickstart.ipynb)
    63	
    64	```python
    65	import ...
    66	
    67	# Define an objective function to be minimized.
    68	def objective(trial):
    69	
    70	    # Invoke suggest methods of a Trial object to generate hyperparameters.
    71	    regressor_name = trial.suggest_categorical('classifier', ['SVR', 'RandomForest'])
    72	    if regressor_name == 'SVR':
    73	        svr_c = trial.suggest_float('svr_c', 1e-10, 1e10, log=True)
    74	        regressor_obj = sklearn.svm.SVR(C=svr_c)
    75	    else:
    76	        rf_max_depth = trial.suggest_int('rf_max_depth', 2, 32)
    77	        regressor_obj = sklearn.ensemble.RandomForestRegressor(max_depth=rf_max_depth)
    78	
    79	    X, y = sklearn.datasets.fetch_california_housing(return_X_y=True)
    80	    X_train, X_val, y_train, y_val = sklearn.model_selection.train_test_split(X, y, random_state=0)
    81	
    82	    regressor_obj.fit(X_train, y_train)
    83	    y_pred = regressor_obj.predict(X_val)
    84	
    85	    error = sklearn.metrics.mean_squared_error(y_val, y_pred)
    86	
    87	    return error  # An objective value linked with the Trial object.
    88	
    89	study = optuna.create_study()  # Create a new study.
    90	study.optimize(objective, n_trials=100)  # Invoke optimization of the objective function.
    91	```

    40	class CmaEsSampler(BaseSampler):
    41	    """A sampler using `cmaes <https://github.com/CyberAgentAILab/cmaes>`_ as the backend.
    42	
    43	    Example:
    44	
    45	        Optimize a simple quadratic function by using :class:`~optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler`.
    46	
    47	        .. testcode::
    48	
    49	            import optuna
    50	
    51	
    52	            def objective(trial):
    53	                x = trial.suggest_float("x", -1, 1)
    54	                y = trial.suggest_int("y", -1, 1)
    55	                return x**2 + y
    56	
    57	
    58	            sampler = optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler()
    59	            study = optuna.create_study(sampler=sampler)
    60	            study.optimize(objective, n_trials=20)
    61	
    62	    Please note that this sampler does not support CategoricalDistribution.
    63	    However, :class:`~optuna.distributions.DiscreteUniformDistribution`
    64	    (:func:`~optuna.trial.Trial.suggest_float`) and
    65	    Int(Log)Distribution (:func:`~optuna.trial.Trial.suggest_int`) are supported.
    66	
    67	    If your search space contains categorical parameters, I recommend you
    68	    to use :class:`~optuna.samplers.TPESampler` instead.
    69	    Furthermore, there is room for performance improvements in parallel
    70	    optimization settings. This sampler cannot use some trials for updating
    71	    the parameters of multivariate normal distribution.
    72	
    73	    For further information about CMA-ES algorithm, please refer to the following papers:
    74	
    75	    - `N. Hansen, The CMA Evolution Strategy: A Tutorial. arXiv:1604.00772, 2016.
    76	      <https://arxiv.org/abs/1604.00772>`_
    77	    - `A. Auger and N. Hansen. A restart CMA evolution strategy with increasing population
    78	      size. In Proceedings of the IEEE Congress on Evolutionary Computation (CEC 2005),
    79	      pages 1769–1776. IEEE Press, 2005.
    80	      <http://www.cmap.polytechnique.fr/~nikolaus.hansen/cec2005ipopcmaes.pdf>`_
    81	    - `Raymond Ros, Nikolaus Hansen. A Simple Modification in CMA-ES Achieving Linear Time and
    82	      Space Complexity. 10th International Conference on Parallel Problem Solving From Nature,
    83	      Sep 2008, Dortmund, Germany. inria-00287367.
    84	      <https://hal.inria.fr/inria-00287367/document>`_
    85	    - `Masahiro Nomura, Shuhei Watanabe, Youhei Akimoto, Yoshihiko Ozaki, Masaki Onishi.
    86	      Warm Starting CMA-ES for Hyperparameter Optimization, AAAI. 2021.
    87	      <https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.06932>`_
    88	
    89	    .. seealso::
    90	        You can also use :class:`optuna.integration.PyCmaSampler` which is a sampler using cma
    91	        library as the backend.
    92	
    93	    Args:
    94	
    95	        x0:
    96	            A dictionary of an initial parameter values for CMA-ES. By default, the mean of ``low``
    97	            and ``high`` for each distribution is used. Note that ``x0`` is sampled uniformly
    98	            within the search space domain for each restart if you specify ``restart_strategy``
    99	            argument.
   100	
   101	        sigma0:
   102	            Initial standard deviation of CMA-ES. By default, ``sigma0`` is set to
   103	            ``min_range / 6``, where ``min_range`` denotes the minimum range of the distributions
   104	            in the search space.
   105	
   106	        seed:
   107	            A random seed for CMA-ES.
   108	
   109	        n_startup_trials:
   110	            The independent sampling is used instead of the CMA-ES algorithm until the given number
   111	            of trials finish in the same study.
   112	
   113	        independent_sampler:
   114	            A :class:`~optuna.samplers.BaseSampler` instance that is used for independent
   115	            sampling. The parameters not contained in the relative search space are sampled
   116	            by this sampler.
   117	            The search space for :class:`~optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler` is determined by
   118	            :func:`~optuna.samplers.intersection_search_space()`.
   119	
   120	            If :obj:`None` is specified, :class:`~optuna.samplers.RandomSampler` is used
   121	            as the default.
   122	
   123	            .. seealso::
   124	                :class:`optuna.samplers` module provides built-in independent samplers
   125	                such as :class:`~optuna.samplers.RandomSampler` and
   126	                :class:`~optuna.samplers.TPESampler`.
   127	
   128	        warn_independent_sampling:
   129	            If this is :obj:`True`, a warning message is emitted when
   130	            the value of a parameter is sampled by using an independent sampler.
   131	
   132	            Note that the parameters of the first trial in a study are always sampled
   133	            via an independent sampler, so no warning messages are emitted in this case.
   134	
   135	        restart_strategy:
   136	            Strategy for restarting CMA-ES optimization when converges to a local minimum.
   137	            If given :obj:`None`, CMA-ES will not restart (default).
   138	            If given 'ipop', CMA-ES will restart with increasing population size.
   139	            Please see also ``inc_popsize`` parameter.
   140	
   141	            .. note::
   142	                Added in v2.1.0 as an experimental feature. The interface may change in newer
   143	                versions without prior notice. See
   144	                https://github.com/optuna/optuna/releases/tag/v2.1.0.
   145	
   146	        inc_popsize:
   147	            Multiplier for increasing population size before each restart.
   148	            This argument will be used when setting ``restart_strategy = 'ipop'``.
   149	
   150	        consider_pruned_trials:
   151	            If this is :obj:`True`, the PRUNED trials are considered for sampling.
   152	
   153	            .. note::
   154	                Added in v2.0.0 as an experimental feature. The interface may change in newer
   155	                versions without prior notice. See
   156	                https://github.com/optuna/optuna/releases/tag/v2.0.0.
   157	
   158	            .. note::
   159	                It is suggested to set this flag :obj:`False` when the
   160	                :class:`~optuna.pruners.MedianPruner` is used. On the other hand, it is suggested
   161	                to set this flag :obj:`True` when the :class:`~optuna.pruners.HyperbandPruner` is
   162	                used. Please see `the benchmark result
   163	                <https://github.com/optuna/optuna/pull/1229>`_ for the details.
   164	
   165	        use_separable_cma:
   166	            If this is :obj:`True`, the covariance matrix is constrained to be diagonal.
   167	            Due to reduce the model complexity, the learning rate for the covariance matrix
   168	            is increased. Consequently, this algorithm outperforms CMA-ES on separable functions.
   169	
   170	            .. note::
   171	                Added in v2.6.0 as an experimental feature. The interface may change in newer
   172	                versions without prior notice. See
   173	                https://github.com/optuna/optuna/releases/tag/v2.6.0.
   174	
   175	        source_trials:
   176	            This option is for Warm Starting CMA-ES, a method to transfer prior knowledge on
   177	            similar HPO tasks through the initialization of CMA-ES. This method estimates a
   178	            promising distribution from ``source_trials`` and generates the parameter of
   179	            multivariate gaussian distribution. Please note that it is prohibited to use
   180	            ``x0``, ``sigma0``, or ``use_separable_cma`` argument together.
   181	
   182	            .. note::
   183	                Added in v2.6.0 as an experimental feature. The interface may change in newer
   184	                versions without prior notice. See
   185	                https://github.com/optuna/optuna/releases/tag/v2.6.0.
   186	
   187	    Raises:
   188	        ValueError:
   189	            If ``restart_strategy`` is not 'ipop' or :obj:`None`.
   190	    """
   191	
   192	    def __init__(

   194	        x0: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,

   219

   223	                " The interface can change in the future.",
   224	                ExperimentalWarning,
   225	            )
   226	
   227	        if self._consider_pruned_trials:

   229	                "`consider_pruned_trials` option is an experimental feature."
   230	                " The interface can change in the future.",
   231	                ExperimentalWarning,
   232	            )
   233	
   234	        if self._use_separable_cma:

   238	                ExperimentalWarning,

   240	
   241	        if self._source_trials is not None:
   242	            warnings.warn(
   243	                "`source_trials` option is an experimental feature."
   244	                " The interface can change in the future.",
   245	                ExperimentalWarning,
   246	            )
   247	
   248	        if source_trials is not None and (x0 is not None or sigma0 is not None):

   262	            "ipop",
   263	            None,
   264	        ):
   265	            raise ValueError(
   266	                "restart_strategy={} is unsupported. Please specify: 'ipop' or None.".format(
   267	                    restart_strategy
   268	                )
   269	            )

   271	    def reseed_rng(self) -> None:
   272	        # _cma_rng doesn't require reseeding because the relative sampling reseeds in each trial.
   273	        self._independent_sampler.reseed_rng()
   274	
   275	    def infer_relative_search_space(
   276	        self, study: "optuna.Study", trial: "optuna.trial.FrozenTrial"
   277	    ) -> Dict[str, BaseDistribution]:
   278	        search_space: Dict[str, BaseDistribution] = {}
   279	        for name, distribution in self._search_space.calculate(study).items():
   280	            if distribution.single():
   281	                # `cma` cannot handle distributions that contain just a single value, so we skip
   282	                # them. Note that the parameter values for such distributions are sampled in
   283	                # `Trial`.
   284	                continue
   285	
   286	            if not isinstance(
   287	                distribution,
   288	                (
   289	                    optuna.distributions.UniformDistribution,
   290	                    optuna.distributions.LogUniformDistribution,
   291	                    optuna.distributions.DiscreteUniformDistribution,
   292	                    optuna.distributions.IntUniformDistribution,
   293	                    optuna.distributions.IntLogUniformDistribution,
   294	                    optuna.distributions.FloatDistribution,
   295	                    optuna.distributions.IntDistribution,
   296	                ),
   297	            ):
   298	                # Categorical distribution is unsupported.
   299	                continue
   300	            search_space[name] = distribution
   301	
   302	        return search_space
   303	
   304	    def sample_relative(
   305	        self,
   306	        study: "optuna.Study",
   307	        trial: "optuna.trial.FrozenTrial",
   308	        search_space: Dict[str, BaseDistribution],
   309	    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   310	
   311	        self._raise_error_if_multi_objective(study)
   312	
   313	        if len(search_space) == 0:
   314	            return {}
   315	
   316	        completed_trials = self._get_trials(study)
   317	        if len(completed_trials) < self._n_startup_trials:
   318	            return {}
   319	
   320	        if len(search_space) == 1:
   321	            _logger.info(
   322	                "`CmaEsSampler` only supports two or more dimensional continuous "
   323	                "search space. `{}` is used instead of `CmaEsSampler`.".format(
   324	                    self._independent_sampler.__class__.__name__
   325	                )
   326	            )
   327	            self._warn_independent_sampling = False
   328	            return {}
   329	
   330	        trans = _SearchSpaceTransform(search_space)
   331	
   332	        optimizer, n_restarts = self._restore_optimizer(completed_trials)
   333	        if optimizer is None:
   334	            n_restarts = 0
   335	            optimizer = self._init_optimizer(trans, study.direction)
   336	
   337	        if self._restart_strategy is None:
   338	            generation_attr_key = "cma:generation"  # for backward compatibility
   339	        else:
   340	            generation_attr_key = "cma:restart_{}:generation".format(n_restarts)
   341	
   342	        if optimizer.dim != len(trans.bounds):
   343	            _logger.info(
   344	                "`CmaEsSampler` does not support dynamic search space. "
   345	                "`{}` is used instead of `CmaEsSampler`.".format(
   346	                    self._independent_sampler.__class__.__name__
   347	                )
   348	            )
   349	            self._warn_independent_sampling = False
   350	            return {}
   351	
   352	        # TODO(c-bata): Reduce the number of wasted trials during parallel optimization.
   353	        # See https://github.com/optuna/optuna/pull/920#discussion_r385114002 for details.
   354	        solution_trials = [
   355	            t
   356	            for t in completed_trials
   357	            if optimizer.generation == t.system_attrs.get(generation_attr_key, -1)
   358	        ]
   359	        if len(solution_trials) >= optimizer.population_size:
   360	            solutions: List[Tuple[np.ndarray, float]] = []
   361	            for t in solution_trials[: optimizer.population_size]:
   362	                assert t.value is not None, "completed trials must have a value"
   363	                x = trans.transform(t.params)
   364	                y = t.value if study.direction == StudyDirection.MINIMIZE else -t.value
   365	                solutions.append((x, y))
   366	
   367	            optimizer.tell(solutions)
   368	
   369	            if self._restart_strategy == "ipop" and optimizer.should_stop():
   370	                n_restarts += 1
   371	                generation_attr_key = "cma:restart_{}:generation".format(n_restarts)
   372	                popsize = optimizer.population_size * self._inc_popsize
   373	                optimizer = self._init_optimizer(
   374	                    trans, study.direction, population_size=popsize, randomize_start_point=True
   375	                )
   376	
   377	            # Store optimizer
   378	            optimizer_str = pickle.dumps(optimizer).hex()
   379	            optimizer_attrs = _split_optimizer_str(optimizer_str)
   380	            for key in optimizer_attrs:
   381	                study._storage.set_trial_system_attr(trial._trial_id, key, optimizer_attrs[key])
   382	
   383	        # Caution: optimizer should update its seed value
   384	        seed = self._cma_rng.randint(1, 2**16) + trial.number
   385	        optimizer._rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

   384	        seed = self._cma_rng.randint(1, 2**16) + trial.number

   386	        params = optimizer.ask()

   397	    def _restore_optimizer(
   398	        self,
   399	        completed_trials: "List[optuna.trial.FrozenTrial]",
   400	    ) -> Tuple[Optional[CmaClass], int]:
   401	        if not self._use_separable_cma:
   402	            attr_key_optimizer = "cma:optimizer"
   403	            attr_key_n_restarts = "cma:n_restarts"
   404	        else:
   405	            attr_key_optimizer = "sepcma:optimizer"
   406	            attr_key_n_restarts = "sepcma:n_restarts"
   407	
   408	        # Restore a previous CMA object.
   409	        for trial in reversed(completed_trials):
   410	            optimizer_attrs = {
   411	                key: value
   412	                for key, value in trial.system_attrs.items()
   413	                if key.startswith(attr_key_optimizer)
   414	            }
   415	            if len(optimizer_attrs) == 0:
   416	                continue
   417	
   418	            if not self._use_separable_cma and "cma:optimizer" in optimizer_attrs:
   419	                # Check "cma:optimizer" key for backward compatibility.
   420	                optimizer_str = optimizer_attrs["cma:optimizer"]
   421	            else:
   422	                optimizer_str = _concat_optimizer_attrs(optimizer_attrs)
   423	
   424	            n_restarts: int = trial.system_attrs.get(attr_key_n_restarts, 0)
   425	            return pickle.loads(bytes.fromhex(optimizer_str)), n_restarts
   426	        return None, 0
   427	
   428	    def _init_optimizer(
   429	        self,
   430	        trans: _SearchSpaceTransform,
   431	        direction: StudyDirection,
   432	        population_size: Optional[int] = None,
   433	        randomize_start_point: bool = False,
   434	    ) -> CmaClass:
   435	        lower_bounds = trans.bounds[:, 0]
   436	        upper_bounds = trans.bounds[:, 1]
   437	        n_dimension = len(trans.bounds)
   438	
   439	        if self._source_trials is None:
   440	            if randomize_start_point:
   441	                mean = lower_bounds + (upper_bounds - lower_bounds) * self._cma_rng.rand(
   442	                    n_dimension
   443	                )
   444	            elif self._x0 is None:
   445	                mean = lower_bounds + (upper_bounds - lower_bounds) / 2
   446	            else:
   447	                # `self._x0` is external representations.
   448	                mean = trans.transform(self._x0)
   449	
   450	            if self._sigma0 is None:
   451	                sigma0 = np.min((upper_bounds - lower_bounds) / 6)
   452	            else:
   453	                sigma0 = self._sigma0
   454	
   455	            cov = None
   456	        else:
   457	            expected_states = [TrialState.COMPLETE]
   458	            if self._consider_pruned_trials:
   459	                expected_states.append(TrialState.PRUNED)
   460	
   461	            # TODO(c-bata): Filter parameters by their values instead of checking search space.
   462	            sign = 1 if direction == StudyDirection.MINIMIZE else -1
   463	            source_solutions = [
   464	                (trans.transform(t.params), sign * cast(float, t.value))
   465	                for t in self._source_trials
   466	                if t.state in expected_states
   467	                and _is_compatible_search_space(trans, t.distributions)
   468	            ]
   469	            if len(source_solutions) == 0:
   470	                raise ValueError("No compatible source_trials")
   471	
   472	            # TODO(c-bata): Add options to change prior parameters (alpha and gamma).
   473	            mean, sigma0, cov = get_warm_start_mgd(source_solutions)
   474	
   475	        # Avoid ZeroDivisionError in cmaes.
   476	        sigma0 = max(sigma0, _EPS)
   477	
   478	        if self._use_separable_cma:
   479	            return SepCMA(
   480	                mean=mean,
   481	                sigma=sigma0,
   482	                bounds=trans.bounds,
   483	                seed=self._cma_rng.randint(1, 2**31 - 2),
   484	                n_max_resampling=10 * n_dimension,
   485	                population_size=population_size,
   486	            )
   487	
   488	        return CMA(
   489	            mean=mean,
   490	            sigma=sigma0,
   491	            cov=cov,
   492	            bounds=trans.bounds,
   493	            seed=self._cma_rng.randint(1, 2**31 - 2),
   494	            n_max_resampling=10 * n_dimension,
   495	            population_size=population_size,
   496	        )
   497	
   498	    def sample_independent(
   499	        self,
   500	        study: "optuna.Study",
   501	        trial: "optuna.trial.FrozenTrial",
   502	        param_name: str,
   503	        param_distribution: BaseDistribution,
   504	    ) -> Any:
   505	
   506	        self._raise_error_if_multi_objective(study)
   507	
   508	        if self._warn_independent_sampling:
   509	            complete_trials = self._get_trials(study)
   510	            if len(complete_trials) >= self._n_startup_trials:
   511	                self._log_independent_sampling(trial, param_name)
   512	
   513	        return self._independent_sampler.sample_independent(
   514	            study, trial, param_name, param_distribution
   515	        )
   516	
   517	    def _log_independent_sampling(self, trial: FrozenTrial, param_name: str) -> None:
   518	        _logger.warning(
   519	            "The parameter '{}' in trial#{} is sampled independently "
   520	            "by using `{}` instead of `CmaEsSampler` "
   521	            "(optimization performance may be degraded). "
   522	            "`CmaEsSampler` does not support dynamic search space or `CategoricalDistribution`. "
   523	            "You can suppress this warning by setting `warn_independent_sampling` "
   524	            "to `False` in the constructor of `CmaEsSampler`, "
   525	            "if this independent sampling is intended behavior.".format(
   526	                param_name, trial.number, self._independent_sampler.__class__.__name__
   527	            )
   528	        )
   529	
   530	    def _get_trials(self, study: "optuna.Study") -> List[FrozenTrial]:
   531	        complete_trials = []
   532	        for t in study.get_trials(deepcopy=False):
   533	            if t.state == TrialState.COMPLETE:
   534	                complete_trials.append(t)
   535	            elif (
   536	                t.state == TrialState.PRUNED
   537	                and len(t.intermediate_values) > 0
   538	                and self._consider_pruned_trials
   539	            ):
   540	                _, value = max(t.intermediate_values.items())
   541	                if value is None:
   542	                    continue
   543	                # We rewrite the value of the trial `t` for sampling, so we need a deepcopy.
   544	                copied_t = copy.deepcopy(t)
   545	                copied_t.value = value
   546	                complete_trials.append(copied_t)
   547	        return complete_trials
   548	
   549	    def after_trial(
   550	        self,
   551	        study: "optuna.Study",
   552	        trial: "optuna.trial.FrozenTrial",
   553	        state: TrialState,
   554	        values: Optional[Sequence[float]],
   555	    ) -> None:
   556	
   557	        self._independent_sampler.after_trial(study, trial, state, values)
   558	
   559	
   560	def _split_optimizer_str(optimizer_str: str) -> Dict[str, str]:
   561	    optimizer_len = len(optimizer_str)
   562	    attrs = {}
   563	    for i in range(math.ceil(optimizer_len / _SYSTEM_ATTR_MAX_LENGTH)):
   564	        start = i * _SYSTEM_ATTR_MAX_LENGTH
   565	        end = min((i + 1) * _SYSTEM_ATTR_MAX_LENGTH, optimizer_len)
   566	        attrs["cma:optimizer:{}".format(i)] = optimizer_str[start:end]
   567	    return attrs
   568	
   569	
   570	def _is_compatible_search_space(
   571	    trans: _SearchSpaceTransform, search_space: Dict[str, BaseDistribution]
   572	) -> bool:
   573	    intersection_size = len(set(trans._search_space.keys()).intersection(search_space.keys()))
   574	    return intersection_size == len(trans._search_space) == len(search_space)
   575	
   576	
   577	def _concat_optimizer_attrs(optimizer_attrs: Dict[str, str]) -> str:
   578	    return "".join(
   579	        optimizer_attrs["cma:optimizer:{}".format(i)] for i in range(len(optimizer_attrs))
   580	    )

     2	import math
     3	from typing import Any

     9	import numpy
    10	
    11	from optuna.distributions import BaseDistribution
    12	from optuna.distributions import CategoricalDistribution
    13	from optuna.distributions import DiscreteUniformDistribution
    14	from optuna.distributions import FloatDistribution
    15	from optuna.distributions import IntDistribution
    16	from optuna.distributions import IntLogUniformDistribution
    17	from optuna.distributions import IntUniformDistribution

    19	from optuna.distributions import UniformDistribution

    21	
    22	class _SearchSpaceTransform:
    23	    """Transform a search space and parameter configurations to continuous space.
    24	
    25	    The search space bounds and parameter configurations are represented as ``numpy.ndarray``s and
    26	    transformed into continuous space. Bounds and parameters associated with categorical
    27	    distributions are one-hot encoded. Parameter configurations in this space can additionally be
    28	    untransformed, or mapped back to the original space. This type of
    29	    transformation/untransformation is useful for e.g. implementing samplers without having to
    30	    condition on distribution types before sampling parameter values.
    31	
    32	    Args:
    33	        search_space:
    34	            The search space. If any transformations are to be applied, parameter configurations
    35	            are assumed to hold parameter values for all of the distributions defined in this
    36	            search space. Otherwise, assertion failures will be raised.
    37	        transform_log:
    38	            If :obj:`True`, apply log/exp operations to the bounds and parameters with
    39	            corresponding distributions in log space during transformation/untransformation.
    40	            Should always be :obj:`True` if any parameters are going to be sampled from the
    41	            transformed space.
    42	        transform_step:
    43	            If :obj:`True`, offset the lower and higher bounds by a half step each, increasing the
    44	            space by one step. This allows fair sampling for values close to the bounds.
    45	            Should always be :obj:`True` if any parameters are going to be sampled from the
    46	            transformed space.
    47	
    48	    Attributes:
    49	        bounds:
    50	            Constructed bounds from the given search space.
    51	        column_to_encoded_columns:
    52	            Constructed mapping from original parameter column index to encoded column indices.
    53	        encoded_column_to_column:
    54	            Constructed mapping from encoded column index to original parameter column index.
    55	
    56	    Note:
    57	        Parameter values are not scaled to the unit cube.
    58	
    59	    Note:
    60	        ``transform_log`` and ``transform_step`` are useful for constructing bounds and parameters
    61	        without any actual transformations by setting those arguments to :obj:`False`. This is
    62	        needed for e.g. the hyperparameter importance assessments.
    63	
    64	    """
    65	
    66	    def __init__(
    67	        self,
    68	        search_space: Dict[str, BaseDistribution],
    69	        transform_log: bool = True,
    70	        transform_step: bool = True,
    71	    ) -> None:
    72	        # TODO(hvy): Avoid casting to `OrderedDict` when Python 3.6 is no longer supported since
    73	        # order will be guaranteed.
    74	        search_space = OrderedDict(search_space)
    75	
    76	        bounds, column_to_encoded_columns, encoded_column_to_column = _transform_search_space(
    77	            search_space, transform_log, transform_step
    78	        )
    79	
    80	        self._bounds = bounds
    81	        self._column_to_encoded_columns = column_to_encoded_columns
    82	        self._encoded_column_to_column = encoded_column_to_column
    83	        self._search_space = search_space
    84	        self._transform_log = transform_log
    85	
    86	    @property
    87	    def bounds(self) -> numpy.ndarray:
    88	        return self._bounds
    89	
    90	    @property
    91	    def column_to_encoded_columns(self) -> List[numpy.ndarray]:
    92	        return self._column_to_encoded_columns
    93	
    94	    @property
    95	    def encoded_column_to_column(self) -> numpy.ndarray:
    96	        return self._encoded_column_to_column
    97	
    98	    def transform(self, params: Dict[str, Any]) -> numpy.ndarray:
    99	        """Transform a parameter configuration from actual values to continuous space.
   100	
   101	        Args:
   102	            params:
   103	                A parameter configuration to transform.
   104	
   105	        Returns:
   106	            A 1-dimensional ``numpy.ndarray`` holding the transformed parameters in the
   107	            configuration.
   108	
   109	        """
   110	        trans_params = numpy.zeros(self._bounds.shape[0], dtype=numpy.float64)
   111	
   112	        bound_idx = 0
   113	        for name, distribution in self._search_space.items():
   114	            assert name in params, "Parameter configuration must contain all distributions."
   115	            param = params[name]
   116	
   117	            if isinstance(distribution, CategoricalDistribution):
   118	                choice_idx = distribution.to_internal_repr(param)
   119	                trans_params[bound_idx + choice_idx] = 1
   120	                bound_idx += len(distribution.choices)
   121	            else:
   122	                trans_params[bound_idx] = _transform_numerical_param(
   123	                    param, distribution, self._transform_log
   124	                )
   125	                bound_idx += 1
   126	
   127	        return trans_params
   128	
   129	    def untransform(self, trans_params: numpy.ndarray) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   130	        """Untransform a parameter configuration from continuous space to actual values.
   131	
   132	        Args:
   133	            trans_params:
   134	                A 1-dimensional ``numpy.ndarray`` in the transformed space corresponding to a
   135	                parameter configuration.
   136	
   137	        Returns:
   138	            A dictionary of an untransformed parameter configuration. Keys are parameter names.
   139	            Values are untransformed parameter values.
   140	
   141	        """
   142	        assert trans_params.shape == (self._bounds.shape[0],)
   143	
   144	        params = {}
   145	
   146	        for (name, distribution), encoded_columns in zip(
   147	            self._search_space.items(), self.column_to_encoded_columns
   148	        ):
   149	            trans_param = trans_params[encoded_columns]
   150	
   151	            if isinstance(distribution, CategoricalDistribution):
   152	                # Select the highest rated one-hot encoding.
   153	                param = distribution.to_external_repr(trans_param.argmax())
   154	            else:
   155	                param = _untransform_numerical_param(
   156	                    trans_param.item(), distribution, self._transform_log
   157	                )
   158	
   159	            params[name] = param
   160	
   161	        return params
   162	
   163	
   164	def _transform_search_space(
   165	    search_space: Dict[str, BaseDistribution], transform_log: bool, transform_step: bool
   166	) -> Tuple[numpy.ndarray, List[numpy.ndarray], numpy.ndarray]:
   167	    assert len(search_space) > 0, "Cannot transform if no distributions are given."
   168	
   169	    n_bounds = sum(
   170	        len(d.choices) if isinstance(d, CategoricalDistribution) else 1
   171	        for d in search_space.values()
   172	    )
   173	
   174	    bounds = numpy.empty((n_bounds, 2), dtype=numpy.float64)
   175	    column_to_encoded_columns: List[numpy.ndarray] = []
   176	    encoded_column_to_column = numpy.empty(n_bounds, dtype=numpy.int64)
   177	
   178	    bound_idx = 0
   179	    for distribution in search_space.values():
   180	        d = distribution
   181	        if isinstance(d, CategoricalDistribution):
   182	            n_choices = len(d.choices)
   183	            bounds[bound_idx : bound_idx + n_choices] = (0, 1)  # Broadcast across all choices.
   184	            encoded_columns = numpy.arange(bound_idx, bound_idx + n_choices)
   185	            encoded_column_to_column[encoded_columns] = len(column_to_encoded_columns)
   186	            column_to_encoded_columns.append(encoded_columns)
   187	            bound_idx += n_choices
   188	        elif isinstance(
   189	            d,
   190	            (
   191	                UniformDistribution,
   192	                LogUniformDistribution,
   193	                DiscreteUniformDistribution,
   194	                IntUniformDistribution,
   195	                IntLogUniformDistribution,
   196	                FloatDistribution,
   197	                IntDistribution,
   198	            ),
   199	        ):
   200	            if isinstance(d, UniformDistribution):
   201	                bds = (
   202	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.low, d, transform_log),
   203	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.high, d, transform_log),
   204	                )
   205	            elif isinstance(d, LogUniformDistribution):
   206	                bds = (
   207	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.low, d, transform_log),
   208	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.high, d, transform_log),
   209	                )
   210	            elif isinstance(d, DiscreteUniformDistribution):
   211	                half_step = 0.5 * d.q if transform_step else 0.0
   212	                bds = (
   213	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.low, d, transform_log) - half_step,
   214	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.high, d, transform_log) + half_step,
   215	                )
   216	            elif isinstance(d, FloatDistribution):
   217	                if d.step is not None:
   218	                    half_step = 0.5 * d.step if transform_step else 0.0
   219	                    bds = (
   220	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.low, d, transform_log) - half_step,
   221	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.high, d, transform_log) + half_step,
   222	                    )
   223	                else:
   224	                    bds = (
   225	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.low, d, transform_log),
   226	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.high, d, transform_log),
   227	                    )
   228	            elif isinstance(d, IntUniformDistribution):
   229	                half_step = 0.5 * d.step if transform_step else 0.0
   230	                bds = (
   231	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.low, d, transform_log) - half_step,
   232	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.high, d, transform_log) + half_step,
   233	                )
   234	            elif isinstance(d, IntLogUniformDistribution):
   235	                half_step = 0.5 if transform_step else 0.0
   236	                bds = (
   237	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.low - half_step, d, transform_log),
   238	                    _transform_numerical_param(d.high + half_step, d, transform_log),
   239	                )
   240	            elif isinstance(d, IntDistribution):
   241	                half_step = 0.5 * d.step if transform_step else 0.0
   242	                if d.log:
   243	                    bds = (
   244	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.low - half_step, d, transform_log),
   245	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.high + half_step, d, transform_log),
   246	                    )
   247	                else:
   248	                    bds = (
   249	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.low, d, transform_log) - half_step,
   250	                        _transform_numerical_param(d.high, d, transform_log) + half_step,
   251	                    )
   252	            else:
   253	                assert False, "Should not reach. Unexpected distribution."
   254	
   255	            bounds[bound_idx] = bds
   256	            encoded_column = numpy.atleast_1d(bound_idx)
   257	            encoded_column_to_column[encoded_column] = len(column_to_encoded_columns)
   258	            column_to_encoded_columns.append(encoded_column)
   259	            bound_idx += 1
   260	        else:
   261	            assert False, "Should not reach. Unexpected distribution."
   262	
   263	    assert bound_idx == n_bounds
   264	
   265	    return bounds, column_to_encoded_columns, encoded_column_to_column
   266	
   267	
   268	def _transform_numerical_param(
   269	    param: Union[int, float], distribution: BaseDistribution, transform_log: bool
   270	) -> float:
   271	    d = distribution
   272	
   273	    if isinstance(d, CategoricalDistribution):
   274	        assert False, "Should not reach. Should be one-hot encoded."
   275	    elif isinstance(d, UniformDistribution):
   276	        trans_param = float(param)
   277	    elif isinstance(d, LogUniformDistribution):
   278	        trans_param = math.log(param) if transform_log else float(param)
   279	    elif isinstance(d, DiscreteUniformDistribution):
   280	        trans_param = float(param)
   281	    elif isinstance(d, FloatDistribution):
   282	        if d.log:
   283	            trans_param = math.log(param) if transform_log else float(param)
   284	        else:
   285	            trans_param = float(param)
   286	    elif isinstance(d, IntUniformDistribution):
   287	        trans_param = float(param)
   288	    elif isinstance(d, IntLogUniformDistribution):
   289	        trans_param = math.log(param) if transform_log else float(param)
   290	    elif isinstance(d, IntDistribution):
   291	        if d.log:
   292	            trans_param = math.log(param) if transform_log else float(param)
   293	        else:
   294	            trans_param = float(param)
   295	    else:
   296	        assert False, "Should not reach. Unexpected distribution."
   297	
   298	    return trans_param
   299	
   300	
   301	def _untransform_numerical_param(
   302	    trans_param: float, distribution: BaseDistribution, transform_log: bool
   303	) -> Union[int, float]:
   304	    d = distribution
   305	
   306	    if isinstance(d, CategoricalDistribution):
   307	        assert False, "Should not reach. Should be one-hot encoded."
   308	    elif isinstance(d, UniformDistribution):
   309	        if d.single():
   310	            param = trans_param
   311	        else:
   312	            param = min(trans_param, numpy.nextafter(d.high, d.high - 1))
   313	    elif isinstance(d, LogUniformDistribution):
   314	        param = math.exp(trans_param) if transform_log else trans_param
   315	        if d.single():
   316	            pass
   317	        else:
   318	            param = min(param, numpy.nextafter(d.high, d.high - 1))
   319	    elif isinstance(d, DiscreteUniformDistribution):
   320	        # Clip since result may slightly exceed range due to round-off errors.
   321	        param = float(
   322	            min(max(numpy.round((trans_param - d.low) / d.q) * d.q + d.low, d.low), d.high)
   323	        )
   324	    elif isinstance(d, FloatDistribution):
   325	        if d.log:
   326	            param = math.exp(trans_param) if transform_log else trans_param
   327	            if d.single():
   328	                pass
   329	            else:
   330	                param = min(param, numpy.nextafter(d.high, d.high - 1))
   331	        elif d.step is not None:
   332	            param = float(
   333	                min(
   334	                    max(numpy.round((trans_param - d.low) / d.step) * d.step + d.low, d.low),
   335	                    d.high,
   336	                )
   337	            )
   338	        else:
   339	            if d.single():
   340	                param = trans_param
   341	            else:
   342	                param = min(trans_param, numpy.nextafter(d.high, d.high - 1))
   343	    elif isinstance(d, IntUniformDistribution):
   344	        param = int(numpy.round((trans_param - d.low) / d.step) * d.step + d.low)
   345	    elif isinstance(d, IntLogUniformDistribution):
   346	        if transform_log:
   347	            param = int(min(max(numpy.round(math.exp(trans_param)), d.low), d.high))
   348	        else:
   349	            param = int(trans_param)
   350	    elif isinstance(d, IntDistribution):
   351	        if d.log:
   352	            if transform_log:
   353	                param = int(min(max(numpy.round(math.exp(trans_param)), d.low), d.high))
   354	            else:
   355	                param = int(trans_param)
   356	        else:
   357	            param = int(numpy.round((trans_param - d.low) / d.step) * d.step + d.low)
   358	    else:
   359	        assert False, "Should not reach. Unexpected distribution."
   360	
   361	    return param
   362

    32	
    33	    This object is passed to an objective function and provides interfaces to get parameter

   105	class FloatDistribution(BaseDistribution):
   106	    """A distribution on floats.
   107	
   108	    This object is instantiated by :func:`~optuna.trial.Trial.suggest_float`, and passed to
   109	    :mod:`~optuna.samplers` in general.
   110	
   111	    .. note::
   112	        When ``step`` is not :obj:`None`, if the range :math:`[\\mathsf{low}, \\mathsf{high}]`
   113	        is not divisible by :math:`\\mathsf{step}`, :math:`\\mathsf{high}` will be replaced
   114	        with the maximum of :math:`k \\times \\mathsf{step} + \\mathsf{low} < \\mathsf{high}`,
   115	        where :math:`k` is an integer.
   116	
   117	    Attributes:
   118	        low:
   119	            Lower endpoint of the range of the distribution. ``low`` is included in the range.
   120	        high:
   121	            Upper endpoint of the range of the distribution. ``high`` is included in the range.
   122	        log:
   123	            If ``log`` is :obj:`True`, this distribution is in log-scaled domain.
   124	        step:
   125	            A discretization step. This parameter must be :obj:`None`
   126	            when the parameter ``log`` is :obj:`True`.
   127	
   128	    Raises:
   129	        ValueError:
   130	            If ``low`` value is larger than ``high`` value.
   131	            If ``log`` is :obj:`True` and ``low`` value is less or equal to 0.0.
   132	            If ``log`` is :obj:`True` and ``step`` is not :obj:`None`.
   133	            If ``step`` is less or equal to 0.
   134	    """
   135	
   136	    def __init__(
   137	        self, low: float, high: float, log: bool = False, step: Union[None, float] = None
   138	    ) -> None:
   139	
   140	        if low > high:
   141	            raise ValueError(
   142	                "The `low` value must be smaller than or equal to the `high` value "
   143	                "(low={}, high={}).".format(low, high)
   144	            )
   145	
   146	        if log and step is not None:
   147	            raise ValueError("The parameter `step` is not supported when `log` is true.")
   148	
   149	        if log and low <= 0.0:
   150	            raise ValueError(
   151	                "The `low` value must be larger than 0 for a log distribution "
   152	                "(low={}, high={}).".format(low, high)
   153	            )
   154	
   155	        if step is not None and step <= 0:
   156	            raise ValueError(
   157	                "The `step` value must be non-zero positive value, " "but step={}.".format(step)
   158	            )
   159	
   160	        self.step = None
   161	        if step is not None:
   162	            high = _adjust_discrete_uniform_high(low, high, step)
   163	            self.step = float(step)
   164	
   165	        self.low = float(low)
   166	        self.high = float(high)
   167	        self.log = log
   168	
   169	    def single(self) -> bool:
   170	
   171	        if self.step is None:
   172	            return self.low == self.high
   173	        else:
   174	            if self.low == self.high:
   175	                return True
   176	            high = decimal.Decimal(str(self.high))
   177	            low = decimal.Decimal(str(self.low))
   178	            step = decimal.Decimal(str(self.step))
   179	            return (high - low) < step
   180	
   181	    def _contains(self, param_value_in_internal_repr: float) -> bool:
   182	
   183	        value = param_value_in_internal_repr
   184	        if self.step is None:
   185	            return self.low <= value <= self.high
   186	        else:
   187	            k = (value - self.low) / self.step
   188	            return self.low <= value <= self.high and abs(k - round(k)) < 1.0e-8
   189	
   190	
   191	@deprecated("3.0.0", "6.0.0", text=_float_distribution_deprecated_msg)
   192	class UniformDistribution(FloatDistribution):
   193	    """A uniform distribution in the linear domain.
   194	
   195	    This object is instantiated by :func:`~optuna.trial.Trial.suggest_float`, and passed to
   196	    :mod:`~optuna.samplers` in general.
   197	
   198	    Attributes:
   199	        low:
   200	            Lower endpoint of the range of the distribution. ``low`` is included in the range.
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I have a clear understanding of the issue. The problem is in the `_contains` method on lines 184-188. The strict inequalities (`<=`) can fail due to floating-point precision errors when CMA-ES samples values that are very close to the bounds but slightly outside.

"""
Reproduction script for CmaEsSampler issue with numerical precision.
"""

import optuna
import numpy as np

def test_cmaes_precision_issue():
    """Test case showing CmaEsSampler triggering independent sampling due to precision errors."""
    
    # Simple objective function
    def objective(trial):
        num = trial.suggest_int("num", 0, 15, step=1)
        denom = trial.suggest_int("denom", 1, 2, step=1)
        x = num / denom
        return 6 * (x**4) - 14 * (x**3) - 2 * (x**2) + 10 * x

    print("Testing CmaEsSampler with numerical precision issues...")
    
    # Set up logging to see warnings
    optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)
    
    # Test multiple seeds to increase chance of reproducing the issue
    for seed in range(20):
        print(f"\nTesting with seed {seed}")
        study = optuna.create_study(
            sampler=optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler(seed=seed)
        )
        try:
            study.optimize(objective, n_trials=100)
            print(f"Best value: {study.best_value:.6f} (params: {study.best_params})")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error with seed {seed}: {e}")

def test_specific_bounds_violation():
    """Test with specific problematic bounds to demonstrate the issue."""

        # These are similar to the bounds mentioned in the issue
        base_filter_size = trial.suggest_int("base_filter_size_ln2", 3, 6, step=1)
        bottleneck_size = trial.suggest_int("bottleneck_size", 10, 150, step=10)
        initial_lr = trial.suggest_float("initial_learning_rate", 1e-05, 0.001, log=True)
        return base_filter_size + bottleneck_size + initial_lr

    print("\n\nTesting with specific bounds from the issue...")

        print(f"\nTesting bounds violation with seed {seed}")

            print(f"Best value: {study.best_value:.6f}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error with seed {seed}: {e}")

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I understand the problem clearly:

1. **Problem Location**: The issue is in the `_contains` methods of distribution classes in `/workspace/optuna__optuna__3.0/optuna/distributions.py`

2. **Root Cause**: CMA-ES algorithm samples values that can be slightly outside the distribution bounds due to floating-point precision errors. For example:
   - `initial_learning_rate`: 9.999e-06 < 1e-05 (slightly below lower bound)
   - `base_filter_size_ln2`: 7 > 6 (slightly above upper bound)

3. **How the Test Reproduces the Issue**: My test script shows that with enough trials, the warnings appear indicating that parameters are being sampled independently instead of using CMA-ES.

4. **Current Behavior**: The `_contains` method uses strict inequalities (`<=`) which fail for values that are epsilon outside the bounds.

5. **Best Practices for the Fix**:
   - Should handle numerical precision gracefully
   - Should not change the fundamental behavior for truly out-of-bounds values
   - Should be minimal and focused
   - Should maintain backward compatibility
   - Should only affect the boundary checking, not the core distribution logic

6. **How to Fix**: 
   - Modify the `_contains` methods to use a small tolerance (epsilon) when checking bounds
   - For continuous distributions, allow values that are slightly outside bounds due to numerical precision
   - For discrete distributions, be more careful since they need exact integer values

Looking at the specific implementations:
- `FloatDistribution._contains` (lines 184-188): Uses `self.low <= value <= self.high`
- `IntDistribution._contains` (lines 385-386): Uses `self.low <= value <= self.high and (value - self.low) % self.step == 0`

The fix should introduce a small tolerance for the boundary checks to account for floating-point precision errors in CMA-ES sampling.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The `CmaEsSampler` frequently triggers independent sampling for non-dynamic search spaces due to floating-point precision errors. CMA-ES generates parameter values that are slightly outside distribution bounds, causing the strict boundary checks in distribution `_contains` methods to fail.

### 5.2 Problem Location  
The issue is in the `_contains` methods of distribution classes in `/workspace/optuna__optuna__3.0/optuna/distributions.py`, specifically:
- `FloatDistribution._contains` (lines 184-188) 
- `IntDistribution._contains` (lines 385-386)

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
The reproduction script shows warnings indicating parameters are sampled independently instead of using CMA-ES when values are slightly outside bounds due to numerical precision errors.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Handle numerical precision gracefully without changing core behavior
- Use minimal, focused changes
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Only affect boundary checking, not core distribution logic
- Introduce small tolerance (epsilon) for floating-point comparisons

### 5.5 Solution Approach
Modify the `_contains` methods to use a small tolerance when checking bounds, allowing values that are slightly outside due to floating-point precision errors while maintaining strict checks for truly invalid values.

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

Let me create a more targeted test to debug this:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/optuna__optuna__3.0/debug_contains.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Debug script to test _contains methods."""

import optuna
from optuna.distributions import IntUniformDistribution, FloatDistribution, LogUniformDistribution

def test_contains_methods():
    """Test the _contains methods with boundary values."""
    
    print("Testing IntUniformDistribution...")
    dist = IntUniformDistribution(low=3, high=6, step=1)
    print(f"Distribution: {dist}")
    
    # Test exact boundary values
    print(f"_contains(3): {dist._contains(3)}")
    print(f"_contains(6): {dist._contains(6)}")
    
    # Test slightly outside values
    print(f"_contains(2.999999999999999): {dist._contains(2.999999999999999)}")
    print(f"_contains(6.000000000000001): {dist._contains(6.000000000000001)}")
    
    # Test non-integer values
    print(f"_contains(3.1): {dist._contains(3.1)}")
    print(f"_contains(5.9): {dist._contains(5.9)}")
    
    print("\nTesting FloatDistribution...")
    dist_float = FloatDistribution(low=1e-05, high=0.001, log=True)
    print(f"Distribution: {dist_float}")
    
    # Test exact boundary values  
    print(f"_contains(1e-05): {dist_float._contains(1e-05)}")
    print(f"_contains(0.001): {dist_float._contains(0.001)}")
    
    # Test slightly outside values
    print(f"_contains(9.999e-06): {dist_float._contains(9.999e-06)}")
    print(f"_contains(0.0010001): {dist_float._contains(0.0010001)}")
    
    print("\nTesting UniformDistribution...")
    dist_uniform = FloatDistribution(low=0.8, high=0.9999)
    print(f"Distribution: {dist_uniform}")
    
    # Test exact boundary values
    print(f"_contains(0.8): {dist_uniform._contains(0.8)}")
    print(f"_contains(0.9999): {dist_uniform._contains(0.9999)}")

import optuna

def debug_epsilon():

    print(f"Low - eps: {dist_float.low - eps}")

    print(f"Test value <= high + eps: {test_value <= dist_float.high + eps}")

"""Debug epsilon calculations."""

import optuna
from optuna.distributions import FloatDistribution

def debug_epsilon():
    """Debug the epsilon calculations."""
    
    dist_float = FloatDistribution(low=1e-05, high=0.001, log=True)
    print(f"Distribution: {dist_float}")
    print(f"Low: {dist_float.low}")
    print(f"High: {dist_float.high}")
    
    test_value = 9.999e-06
    print(f"\nTest value: {test_value}")
    print(f"Test value < low: {test_value < dist_float.low}")
    print(f"Difference: {dist_float.low - test_value}")
    
    eps = 1e-6
    print(f"Epsilon: {eps}")
    print(f"Low - eps: {dist_float.low - eps}")
    print(f"Test value >= low - eps: {test_value >= dist_float.low - eps}")
    print(f"Test value <= high + eps: {test_value <= dist_float.high + eps}")
    print(f"Combined check: {(dist_float.low - eps <= test_value <= dist_float.high + eps)}")
    
    # Manual check of _contains logic
    eps = 1e-6
    result = dist_float.low - eps <= test_value <= dist_float.high + eps
    print(f"My _contains should return: {result}")
    
    # Actual _contains result
    print(f"Actual _contains result: {dist_float._contains(test_value)}")
    
    # Test the other boundary
    test_value2 = 0.0010001
    print(f"\nTest value 2: {test_value2}")
    print(f"Test value 2 > high: {test_value2 > dist_float.high}")
    print(f"Difference: {test_value2 - dist_float.high}")
    print(f"Test value 2 <= high + eps: {test_value2 <= dist_float.high + eps}")
    print(f"Actual _contains result 2: {dist_float._contains(test_value2)}")

"""
Comprehensive test to verify the fix for CmaEsSampler numerical precision issue.
"""

import optuna
import numpy as np

def test_boundary_tolerance():
    """Test that values slightly outside bounds are accepted."""
    
    print("Testing boundary tolerance...")
    
    # Test integer distribution
    int_dist = optuna.distributions.IntDistribution(low=3, high=6, step=1)
    print(f"Integer distribution: {int_dist}")
    
    # Test slightly outside values that should now be accepted
    assert int_dist._contains(2.999999999999999), "Should accept slightly below lower bound"
    assert int_dist._contains(6.000000000000001), "Should accept slightly above upper bound"
    print("✓ Integer boundary tolerance works")
    
    # Test float distribution with log scale (like in the issue)
    float_dist = optuna.distributions.FloatDistribution(low=1e-05, high=0.001, log=True)
    print(f"Float log distribution: {float_dist}")
    
    # Test slightly outside values that were problematic in the issue
    assert float_dist._contains(9.999e-06), "Should accept slightly below lower bound"
    assert float_dist._contains(0.0010001), "Should accept slightly above upper bound"
    print("✓ Float log boundary tolerance works")
    
    # Test uniform float distribution 
    uniform_dist = optuna.distributions.FloatDistribution(low=0.8, high=0.9999)
    print(f"Uniform distribution: {uniform_dist}")
    
    # Test values from the issue
    assert uniform_dist._contains(0.7999999999999999), "Should accept slightly below lower bound"
    assert uniform_dist._contains(0.9998999999999999), "Should accept boundary value"
    print("✓ Uniform boundary tolerance works")

def test_original_issue_scenario():
    """Test the exact scenario from the original issue."""
    
    print("\nTesting original issue scenario...")
    
    def objective(trial):
        # These mimic the distributions from the issue
        initial_lr = trial.suggest_float("initial_learning_rate", 1e-05, 0.001, log=True)
        decay_factor = trial.suggest_float("learning_rate_decay_factor", 0.8, 0.9999)
        filter_size = trial.suggest_int("base_filter_size_ln2", 3, 6, step=1)
        block_size = trial.suggest_int("block_size", 1, 2, step=1)
        bottleneck_size = trial.suggest_int("bottleneck_size", 10, 150, step=10)
        num_blocks = trial.suggest_int("num_blocks", 2, 5, step=1)
        downsampling = trial.suggest_int("num_downsampling_steps", 3, 7, step=1)
        reg_scale = trial.suggest_float("reg_scale_l1", 0.0001, 0.1, log=True)
        epochs = trial.suggest_int("number_of_epochs", 500, 1000, step=100)
        weight_decay = trial.suggest_float("weight_decay", 1e-06, 0.001, log=True)
        
        # Simple objective function
        return (initial_lr + decay_factor + filter_size + block_size + 
                bottleneck_size + num_blocks + downsampling + reg_scale + 
                epochs + weight_decay)

    # Test with multiple seeds to increase chance of hitting boundary conditions
    warnings_count = 0
    total_trials = 0
    
    for seed in range(5):  # Test with 5 different seeds
        optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)  # Suppress INFO messages
        
        study = optuna.create_study(
            sampler=optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler(seed=seed, warn_independent_sampling=True)
        )
        
        # Capture warnings by temporarily redirecting stderr
        import io
        import sys
        old_stderr = sys.stderr
        sys.stderr = io.StringIO()
        
        try:
            study.optimize(objective, n_trials=50)
            total_trials += 50
            
            # Get captured warnings
            warnings_output = sys.stderr.getvalue()
            warning_lines = warnings_output.count("sampled independently")
            warnings_count += warning_lines
            
        finally:
            sys.stderr = old_stderr

    print(f"Total trials: {total_trials}")
    print(f"Independent sampling warnings: {warnings_count}")
    
    # The fix should significantly reduce warnings
    if warnings_count < total_trials * 0.1:  # Less than 10% of trials should trigger warnings
        print("✓ Boundary tolerance fix is working - warnings significantly reduced")
        return True
    else:
        print("! Warning: Still getting many warnings, fix may need adjustment")
        return False

def test_edge_cases():
    """Test edge cases to ensure we don't break legitimate bounds checking."""
    
    print("\nTesting edge cases...")
    
    # Test that truly out-of-bounds values are still rejected
    int_dist = optuna.distributions.IntDistribution(low=1, high=10, step=1)
    
    # These should still be rejected
    assert not int_dist._contains(0), "Should reject clearly below lower bound"
    assert not int_dist._contains(11), "Should reject clearly above upper bound"
    assert not int_dist._contains(1.5), "Should reject non-integer values"
    
    float_dist = optuna.distributions.FloatDistribution(low=0.0, high=1.0)
    
    # These should still be rejected (well outside bounds)
    assert not float_dist._contains(-1.0), "Should reject clearly below lower bound"
    assert not float_dist._contains(2.0), "Should reject clearly above upper bound"
    
    print("✓ Edge cases handled correctly")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_boundary_tolerance()
    success = test_original_issue_scenario()
    test_edge_cases()
    
    if success:
        print("\n🎉 All tests passed! The fix successfully addresses the numerical precision issue.")
    else:
        print("\n⚠ Some issues remain. """
Final verification that the fix works for the exact issue reported.
"""

import optuna

def test_exact_mwe_from_issue():
    """Test the exact MWE from the GitHub issue."""
    
    class MWE:
        def __call__(self, trial):
            num = trial.suggest_int("num", 0, 15, step=1)
            denom = trial.suggest_int("denom", 1, 2, step=1)
            x = num / denom
            return 6 * (x**4) - 14 * (x**3) - 2 * (x**2) + 10 * x

    print("Testing the exact MWE from the issue...")
    print("This should produce fewer warnings with the fix.")
    
    optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)
    objective = MWE()

    # Test with the exact parameters from the issue report
    for seed in range(20):
        study = optuna.create_study(
            sampler=optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler(seed=seed)
        )
        study.optimize(objective, n_trials=100)
        if seed < 3:  # Just show first few results
            print(f"Seed {seed}: Best value: {study.best_value:.6f} (params: {study.best_params})")

def test_complex_scenario():
    """Test a more complex scenario similar to what was reported."""
    
    def objective(trial):
        # Recreate the exact distributions from the issue
        initial_lr = trial.suggest_float("initial_learning_rate", 1e-05, 0.001, log=True)
        decay_factor = trial.suggest_float("learning_rate_decay_factor", 0.8, 0.9999)
        filter_size = trial.suggest_int("base_filter_size_ln2", 3, 6, step=1)
        block_size = trial.suggest_int("block_size", 1, 2, step=1)
        bottleneck_size = trial.suggest_int("bottleneck_size", 10, 150, step=10)
        num_blocks = trial.suggest_int("num_blocks", 2, 5, step=1)
        downsampling = trial.suggest_int("num_downsampling_steps", 3, 7, step=1)
        reg_scale = trial.suggest_float("reg_scale_l1", 0.0001, 0.1, log=True)
        epochs = trial.suggest_int("number_of_epochs", 500, 1000, step=100)
        weight_decay = trial.suggest_float("weight_decay", 1e-06, 0.001, log=True)
        
        # Simple composite objective
        return (initial_lr * 1000 + decay_factor + filter_size + block_size + 
                bottleneck_size/100 + num_blocks + downsampling + reg_scale * 10000 +
                epochs/1000 + weight_decay * 1000000)

    print("\nTesting complex scenario with exact distributions from issue...")
    
    optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)
    
    # Run multiple times to get a sense of warning frequency
    total_warnings = 0
    import sys
    import io
    
    for seed in range(5):
        # Capture stderr to count warnings
        old_stderr = sys.stderr
        sys.stderr = io.StringIO()
        
        try:
            study = optuna.create_study(
                sampler=optuna.samplers.CmaEsSampler(seed=seed, warn_independent_sampling=True)
            )
            study.optimize(objective, n_trials=50)
            
            # Count warnings
            stderr_content = sys.stderr.getvalue()
            warning_count = stderr_content.count("sampled independently")
            total_warnings += warning_count
            
            if warning_count > 0:
                print(f"Seed {seed}: {warning_count} warnings")
                
        finally:
            sys.stderr = old_stderr
    
    print(f"Total warnings across 5 seeds (250 trials): {total_warnings}")
    
    # Before the fix, we would expect many more warnings
    if total_warnings < 25:  # Less than 10% of trials
        print("✅ SUCCESS: Numerical precision fix is working!")
        print("   Warnings are significantly reduced compared to the original issue.")
    else:
        print("⚠️  CONCERN: Still getting many warnings.")
        print("   May need further adjustment.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_exact_mwe_from_issue()
